## 7:105 — حَقِيقٌ عَلَىٰٓ أَن لَّآ أَقُولَ عَلَى ٱللَّهِ إِلَّا ٱلْحَقَّ ۚ قَدْ جِئْتُكُم بِبَيِّنَةٍۢ مِّن رَّبِّكُمْ فَأَرْسِلْ مَعِىَ بَنِىٓ إِسْرَٰٓءِيلَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ح ق ق:B002` (due obligation and deserved claim) governs speaking only `B001` (firm truth).** Moses binds his speech-role to an obligation not to attribute falsely, reversing the unsupported divine attributions condemned earlier. I infer commission → speech constraint. Truth is a duty before it is a weapon.
2. **`ب ي ن:B004` (appearance and becoming clear) arrives from the Lord, then grounds a political imperative.** I infer demonstrative proof → warranted claim upon Pharaoh's action. The sign is not spectacle; it licenses release.
3. **`ر س ل:B001` (release/sending forth) links Moses' messenger-status to `فَأَرْسِلْ` “release.”** A released envoy from universal lordship asks the local ruler to release a people. I infer true commission breaks unauthorized containment. The repeated root makes liberation the social translation of messengerhood.
4. **`ب ن ي:B007` (filiation and derived belonging).** “Children of Israel” identifies a lineage-bound collective whose belonging Pharaoh's regime cannot overwrite. I infer prior filiation supports their claim to leave imposed ownership. The demand restores a suppressed relation.

### Retrospective surprises

The messenger in 7:157 later removes burdens and shackles, making liberation a general consequence of truthful commission rather than a one-cycle political demand. The actual inheritance in 7:137 confirms that release can restructure land and rule while still leaving higher ownership intact.

## 7:106 — قَالَ إِن كُنتَ جِئْتَ بِـَٔايَةٍۢ فَأْتِ بِهَآ إِن كُنتَ مِنَ ٱلصَّٰدِقِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ء ي ي:B003` (visible sign), `ج ي ء:B004` (bringing/presenting), and `ء ت ي:B002` (granting/bringing).** Pharaoh demands that the claimed evidence be moved from reported possession into shared visibility. I infer public presentation becomes the agreed test condition. The sentence temporarily establishes an evidentiary protocol.
2. **`ص د ق:B004` (confirmation and fulfillment).** The sign is requested as confirmation of Moses' truthful status, not merely as marvel. I infer correspondence between claim and displayed event → credibility. The challenge makes the subsequent reclassification as magic a breach of Pharaoh's own test.
3. **`ك و ن:B001` (being/occurrence) in both conditionals.** Moses' status is made conditional on an event that can occur before observers. I infer the court asks ontology to adjudicate speech. The next two ayat satisfy that event condition.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:132 later exposes the breach of this agreed protocol: Pharaoh's people declare in advance that any sign brought to them will be classified as magic and will not produce trust. The subsequent distinct signs in 7:133 confirm that failure lies in a moving criterion, not in non-fulfillment of the requested public test.

## 7:107 — فَأَلْقَىٰ عَصَاهُ فَإِذَا هِىَ ثُعْبَانٌۭ مُّبِينٌۭ

### Activated readings

1. **`ل ق ي:B005` (casting something where it is met) acts on `ع ص و:B001` (working staff).** An ordinary instrument is put into the public test-space and becomes a large serpent. I infer relinquishing hand-control → transformation becomes inspectable. The sign begins with a work-tool leaving private possession.
2. **`ث ع ب:B002` (large extended serpent), with `B001` (water bursting through a channel) retained exploratorily.** The serpent's extended, channel-like movement is later able to catch the magicians' diverted production. I infer released extension → active traversal of the contest field. The water-channel image is secondary because no water is supplied in this immediate window.
3. **`ب ي ن:B004` (appearance and becoming clear).** The serpent is manifest immediately after Pharaoh demanded a visible sign and before the elite call it magic. I infer clarity precedes and is not created by their classification. `مُبِينٌ` secures the public datum against later reframing.

### Retrospective surprises

The many distinct signs in 7:133 later show that manifestness alone cannot prevent a fixed receiver from recycling the magic-label. Ayah 7:160 gives the staff a surprisingly different public role when striking stone releases apportioned water; an ordinary tool under command can expose false production or provision a community.

## 7:108 — وَنَزَعَ يَدَهُۥ فَإِذَا هِىَ بَيْضَآءُ لِلنَّاظِرِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ن ز ع:B001` (pulling something out of its place) reveals `ي د ي:B001` (the bodily hand).** The hand is withdrawn from concealment and offered to viewers, complementing the staff cast away in 7:107. I infer removal from its prior place → public inspectability. Both signs involve relinquishing ordinary bodily/tool placement.
2. **`ب ي ض:B001` (whiteness) with `B002` (white brightness of face and honor).** The brightness is explicitly “for the observers,” while 7:116 later shows magic acting on people's eyes. I infer unmediated visible brightness → evidentiary exposure rather than diverted vision. Honor is displayed without court-granted status.
3. **`ن ظ ر:B001` (directing sight to apprehend).** The observers are invited into active seeing before the elite monopolize interpretation in 7:109. I infer distributed observation threatens centralized classification. The sign is socially public.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:179 later distinguishes having eyes from using them to understand, while 7:198 depicts an apparent gaze without actual sight and 7:201 restores sight through remembrance. These findings qualify public inspectability: the sign is available to observers, but observation remains a practiced capacity rather than an automatic visual result.

## 7:109 — قَالَ ٱلْمَلَأُ مِن قَوْمِ فِرْعَوْنَ إِنَّ هَٰذَا لَسَٰحِرٌ عَلِيمٌۭ

### Activated readings

1. **`م ل ء:B002` (assembled elite council) reclassifies the public signs through `س ح ر:B002` (magic as diversion and deception).** Pharaoh's requested evidence has appeared, but the elite divert attention from its source to a technical category. I infer institutional naming → containment of disruptive proof. The label is a political defense mechanism.
2. **`ع ل م:B001` (knowing as clear) is reassigned to the alleged magician.** Instead of accepting the signs as clear, they explain them through Moses' expert knowledge and then recruit competing experts. I infer attributing skill to producer → naturalization of sign. “Knowing magician” turns proof into a reproducible craft claim.
3. **`ق و ل:B010` (arrogating judgment over someone).** The elite pronounce what Moses “is” without addressing the prior truth obligation or release demand. I infer adjudicative speech substitutes for the agreed sign-test. Their saying seizes interpretive authority.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:132 later turns the elite's local label into an advance policy: whatever sign arrives will be called magic. Ayah 7:146 identifies self-magnification as the condition under which every sign can be seen yet refused, strongly confirming institutional reclassification as status defense rather than an evidence-led diagnosis.

## 7:110 — يُرِيدُ أَن يُخْرِجَكُم مِّنْ أَرْضِكُمْ ۖ فَمَاذَا تَأْمُرُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ر و د:B001` (will and intention) is attributed to Moses without supplied evidence.** His explicit demand concerned releasing the Israelites; the elite infer a hidden motive to expel Pharaoh's people. I distinguish packet speech from their directional arrow release-request → territorial takeover. The ayah exposes motive construction.
2. **`خ ر ج:B002` (causing to come out) from `ء ر ض:B001` (ground/land) reverses Moses' actual release demand.** I infer the court projects its own containment logic: any release from rule is imagined as expulsion of rulers. The political frame converts liberation into invasion.
3. **`ء م ر:B007` (consultation and deliberation), activated in the question.** The elite move from diagnosis to policy design, and 7:111–112 establish a citywide recruitment plan. I infer fear-framing → emergency consultation → institutional counter-production. Interpretation immediately becomes administration.

### Retrospective surprises

Pharaoh repeats and enlarges this projection in 7:123, calling unlicensed belief a citywide expulsion plot, while his council in 7:127 labels liberation “corruption.” The later findings confirm that the regime translates every reduction of its containment power into a fabricated threat to land and social order.

## 7:111 — قَالُوٓا۟ أَرْجِهْ وَأَخَاهُ وَأَرْسِلْ فِى ٱلْمَدَآئِنِ حَٰشِرِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ر ج و:B004` (deferring something) delays Moses and his brother while the court mobilizes.** I infer postponement creates production time for a counter-sign network. The earlier community pattern of waiting is now state strategy.
2. **`ر س ل:B001` (sending forth) is appropriated by the regime.** Pharaoh counters the divine messenger by sending gatherers through cities. I infer competing dispatch systems stage a contest of authorities. One sends signs toward power; the other sends agents outward to consolidate power.
3. **`م د ن:B001` (fortified city) and `ح ش ر:B001` (gathering and driving a group to a destination).** Expertise is extracted from distributed urban centers and driven toward Pharaoh. I infer administrative reach → centralized spectacle. The court manufactures numerical and technical fullness.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:133 later answers the court's distributed recruitment with a sequence of distinct signs sent upon the regime itself. The contrast strengthens the competing-dispatch reading: administrative reach can gather performers and delay decision, but it cannot control what enters the resulting test-field.

## 7:112 — يَأْتُوكَ بِكُلِّ سَٰحِرٍ عَلِيمٍۢ

### Activated readings

1. **`ك ل ل:B003` (totality and completeness) scopes `س ح ر:B002` (diversion/deception) and `ع ل م:B001` (knowledge).** The strategy seeks every available expert, treating proof as a contest won by technical aggregation. I infer maximal recruitment → expected explanatory dominance. Completeness of talent is used as a substitute for truth.
2. **`ء ت ي:B002` (bringing) reverses Moses' singular brought sign.** City agents will bring many experts to answer one messenger's evidence. I infer institutional scale is intended to overwhelm the sign's source claim. The contest pits accumulation against demonstration.
3. **`ع ل م:B004` is absent; `B001` remains the operative branch.** Their knowledge is real technical clarity within magic, but the surrounding mechanism shows domain expertise does not settle the truth-status of its object. I infer expertise can reproduce appearances while misclassifying source.

### Retrospective surprises

The advance refusal in 7:132 and the differentiated signs in 7:133 confirm that maximal domain expertise cannot decide source or truth by accumulation. Ayah 7:146 generalizes the failure: even seeing every sign need not yield guidance when the route-classification is already governed by self-magnification.

## 7:113 — وَجَآءَ ٱلسَّحَرَةُ فِرْعَوْنَ قَالُوٓا۟ إِنَّ لَنَا لَأَجْرًا إِن كُنَّا نَحْنُ ٱلْغَٰلِبِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ء ج ر:B001` (recompense for work) makes the magicians' production explicitly transactional.** They condition performance on payment before discussing truth. I infer reward contract → alignment of expertise with Pharaoh's desired outcome. The court's evidence-production has a wage structure.
2. **`غ ل ب:B001` (overpowering force) is the success criterion.** The question asks whether winning, not correspondence, earns reward; 7:118 later establishes truth by voiding the work. I infer domination metric → substitution of victory for validity. Their epistemic contest is organized as labor for power.
3. **`ج ي ء:B001` (arrival) completes the city-gathering chain.** Dispatch, collection, and arrival show the regime's logistical capacity. I infer centralized arrival produces a staged public field, but not guaranteed outcome. The assembled expertise is causally real even though its claim will fail.

### Retrospective surprises

By 7:126 the same experts have abandoned both wage and victory, asking instead for poured-out patience and a faithful death. Ayah 7:170 later guarantees that the reward of repairers is not wasted. The full run therefore exposes two recompense systems: purchased performance for dominance and accountable action after truth-recognition.

## 7:114 — قَالَ نَعَمْ وَإِنَّكُمْ لَمِنَ ٱلْمُقَرَّبِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ق ر ب:B004` (favor, access, and privileged nearness) is offered in addition to wage.** Pharaoh purchases not only work but loyalty through access to power. I infer promised proximity → intensified incentive to produce the ruler's preferred reality. Nearness is a governance currency.
2. **`ق ر ب:B001` (nearness opposite distance) contrasts with Moses' commission from the Lord of the worlds.** The magicians are invited near a local throne, while the later proof causes them to prostrate to a higher Lord. I infer local access initially organizes allegiance, but source-recognition can reorder proximity. The terse promise sets up that rank reversal.
3. **`ق و ل:B001` turns the employment question into a binding oral contract.** I infer public assent secures the court's side of the performance economy. The single “yes” authorizes both payment and status.

### Retrospective surprises

The magicians' appeal to “our Lord” in 7:126 shows that their rank reversal survives the threatened loss of court access. Ayah 7:196 later defines divine patronage of the sound, confirming that protective relation, rather than physical distance from a throne, is the consequential form of nearness.

## 7:115 — قَالُوا۟ يَٰمُوسَىٰٓ إِمَّآ أَن تُلْقِىَ وَإِمَّآ أَن نَّكُونَ نَحْنُ ٱلْمُلْقِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ل ق ي:B005` (casting something where it is met) is offered under two sequencing options.** The magicians frame the contest as symmetric performance: either Moses casts or they do. I infer control of order is treated as tactical advantage, while both outputs share a public field. The sign is temporarily assimilated to their craft procedure.
2. **`ك و ن:B001` (being/occurrence) in “we be the casters” emphasizes role identity.** Their paid, privileged status is now condensed into the performer role. I infer institutional recruitment → self-identification with production. They stand as the court's casting apparatus.
3. **The repeated root anticipates agency reversal in 7:120.** Those who choose to be “the casters” will themselves be cast down prostrating. I infer the contest's outcome turns controlled action into transformed state. This later local relation sharpens the fixed alternative.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayat 7:125–126 show that the passive reversal anticipated here is not momentary bodily collapse: the former casters reinterpret threatened death as return and ask to remain surrendered. Later evidence thus confirms that an externally overwhelming encounter becomes a durable, chosen orientation.

## 7:116 — قَالَ أَلْقُوا۟ ۖ فَلَمَّآ أَلْقَوْا۟ سَحَرُوٓا۟ أَعْيُنَ ٱلنَّاسِ وَٱسْتَرْهَبُوهُمْ وَجَآءُو بِسِحْرٍ عَظِيمٍۢ

### Activated readings

1. **`س ح ر:B002` (magic as diversion and deception) acts directly on `ع ي ن:B001` (seeing eye).** Unlike Moses' sign “for the observers,” their work manipulates the instrument of observation. I infer diverted perception → appearance detached from source reality. The contest attacks the test apparatus itself.
2. **`ر ه ب:B002` (causing fear and driving off) supplements visual diversion.** They do not only show; they induce recoil. I infer perception manipulation + affective pressure → reduced independent judgment. The spectacle controls both what the crowd sees and how it can respond.
3. **`ع ظ م:B001` (greatness and strength) describes produced magic within the court's domination metric.** Scale and emotional impact make the performance appear evidentially weighty. I infer magnitude → social persuasiveness, not truth. “Great” is an effect-measure.
4. **`ل ق ي:B005` is repeated before `ج ي ء:B004` bringing.** Cast instruments are presented as a finished magical product. I infer coordinated performance transforms many throws into one brought spectacle. Production is collective and staged.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:132 shows the court later using “magic” not only as a technique but as a standing interpretive label for every unwelcome sign. Ayat 7:179 and 7:198 then generalize the vulnerability: eyes may be present or apparently directed while apprehension remains absent. Visual availability therefore needs disciplined uptake.

## 7:117 — ۞ وَأَوْحَيْنَآ إِلَىٰ مُوسَىٰٓ أَنْ أَلْقِ عَصَاكَ ۖ فَإِذَا هِىَ تَلْقَفُ مَا يَأْفِكُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`و ح ي:B001` (hidden imparting of knowledge) and `B004` (divine message) directs one public cast.** Hidden instruction, unlike hidden plot, produces an auditable visible event. I infer source-guided action → response precisely fitted to the deception. Revelation coordinates rather than manipulates perception.
2. **`ل ق ف:B001` (quick catching and taking) acts on what `ء ف ك:B001` turns from proper direction.** The staff does not merely outperform appearances; it rapidly removes the diverted production itself. I infer truthful sign catches and absorbs the mechanism of falsification. False direction loses material standing.
3. **`ع ص و:B001` (working staff) reverses the magicians' many instruments and the root's `B007` refusal-of-obedience branch.** Under command, the staff becomes an obedient work-tool that consumes disobedient distortion. I infer proper instrument-source relation → effective correction. Ordinary toolhood is not displaced but commissioned.
4. **`ل ق ي:B005` now repeats Moses' first cast and the magicians' cast.** Identical action-form permits comparison of unlike outputs. I infer surface procedural symmetry makes causal-source difference more visible. What follows the cast adjudicates it.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:201 later gives an inward analogue: when suggestion touches the cautious, remembrance restores sight. The staff's public removal of diverted production and remembrance's internal correction of diverted perception share a pattern of source-linked recollection making distortion lose its hold.

## 7:118 — فَوَقَعَ ٱلْحَقُّ وَبَطَلَ مَا كَانُوا۟ يَعْمَلُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`و ق ع:B001` (falling into occurrence and fixed reality) makes `ح ق ق:B001` (firm truth) an event.** After manipulated eyes and swallowed false production, truth occupies the shared field as settled fact. I infer removal of distortion → truth's fixed public standing. Truth is not merely asserted more loudly.
2. **`ب ط ل:B002` (making void and removing) acts on `ع م ل:B001` (intentional work).** Their work, recruitment, wages, and spectacle are causally real but lose truth-bearing validity. I infer a production process can be elaborate yet void as evidence. The ayah distinguishes work-existence from claim-validity.
3. **`ح ق ق:B005` (making truth stand) is activated by the surrounding action sequence.** The staff's catching supplies the mechanism by which truth stands and the rival output disappears. I infer demonstrative correction establishes, not invents, truth. The event discloses correspondence.

### Retrospective surprises

The clear signs of 7:133 followed by continued self-magnification, and the “every sign” refusal in 7:146, add an important qualification: truth can stand publicly and false work can be voided without compelling every observer's assent. Ayah 7:181 preserves the positive counterpart, a community that guides and judges by truth.

## 7:119 — فَغُلِبُوا۟ هُنَالِكَ وَٱنقَلَبُوا۟ صَٰغِرِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`غ ل ب:B001` (overpowering force) reverses the magicians' contracted victory criterion.** The entire court apparatus is overcome at the site it designed for dominance. I infer defining truth as victory exposes the regime to its own metric when proof defeats it. The passive marks loss of control.
2. **`ق ل ب:B004` (turning and reversing) leads to `ص غ ر:B002` (abasement).** Privileged proximity and promised reward reverse into lowered status, then 7:120 makes that lowering bodily prostration. I infer evidentiary reversal → rank reversal. Their inward orientation and social position turn together.
3. **`ق ل ب:B001` (heart/inward faculty) is activated by their immediate belief in 7:121.** The same root allows the reversal to be read as inward turning, not defeat alone. I infer direct expertise lets them recognize the difference between craft and sign. This causal expertise-to-recognition arrow is inferred from their sequence.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayat 7:125–126 disclose the depth of the reversal: threatened execution is read as return to the Lord, and former court contractors ask to die surrendered. The later finding confirms inward turning rather than mere social humiliation as the lasting result for the expert performers.

## 7:120 — وَأُلْقِىَ ٱلسَّحَرَةُ سَٰجِدِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ل ق ي:B005` (casting something where it is met), now passive, reverses 7:115–117.** Those who chose to be the casters are themselves “cast” into a new posture. I infer encountered proof overwhelms their prior role agency. The repeated verb turns producer into transformed object.
2. **`س ج د:B001` (lowering in humble submission).** Social abasement in 7:119 becomes deliberate-looking bodily lowering, followed by verbal trust in the Lord of the worlds. I infer recognition of true source → reorientation of allegiance → prostration. Lowering is liberation from Pharaoh's promised nearness.
3. **`س ح ر:B002` (diversion/deception) remains anchored in the subject “the magicians.”** Their expert identity is not erased before response; it enables them to diagnose the voiding of their work. I infer domain knowledge → rapid recognition that the event exceeds their production mechanism. The practitioners become the first public witnesses against the craft's evidentiary claim.

### Retrospective surprises

The response under threat in 7:125–126 shows that being “cast” into prostration does not erase agency; it initiates speech, endurance, and chosen surrender. Later 7:157's release from burdens and shackles also confirms this lowering as liberation from Pharaoh's purchased proximity, not passive transfer to another human controller.

## 7:121 — قَالُوٓا۟ ءَامَنَّا بِرَبِّ ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ء م ن:B002` (trusting assent and confirmation) follows expert recognition and bodily prostration.** The magicians move from paid service to Pharaoh toward trust in the source whose sign voided their craft. I infer technical recognition → reallocated trust. Belief is a public expert verdict as well as inward assent.
2. **`ر ب ب:B001` (lordship, ownership, and authority) over `ع ل م:B002` (distinguishing marks/worlds).** Pharaoh offered wages and privileged nearness; they answer with a universal authority-title that relativizes his court. I infer the sign identifies jurisdiction beyond local patronage. Their allegiance moves from employer to Lord.
3. **`ق و ل:B001` (uttered speech) makes the change auditable.** Their prior work manipulated public sight; their new relation is declared in direct words and specified in 7:122. I infer transparent testimony repairs their former role in deception. Speech becomes counter-evidence against the spectacle they produced.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:158 later repeats the universal lordship claim inside a universal messenger-address, then commands trust and following. Ayah 7:188 limits the messenger's own power, confirming that the experts' verdict reallocates allegiance to the sign's source rather than simply promoting Moses into Pharaoh's place.

## 7:122 — رَبِّ مُوسَىٰ وَهَٰرُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ر ب ب:B001` (lordship and authority) is specified by relation to Moses and Aaron.** The universal title in 7:121 is not left as an abstract power that Pharaoh might appropriate. I infer naming the commissioned pair identifies the authority through its concrete message-history. Specification prevents equivocation.
2. **`ر ب ب:B002` (repair, nurture, and completion).** Moses' and Aaron's Lord has just repaired public perception by voiding deception and reorienting its practitioners. I infer nurturing authority can complete a transformation that coercive patronage could not produce. The genitive relation carries a formation-history.
3. **The extreme brevity functions structurally as correction.** Pharaoh's apparatus was elaborate; the experts answer with one relational phrase. I infer evidentiary clarity reduces the need for performative excess. This reading remains anchored to the repeated lordship construction.

### Retrospective surprises

The messenger's concrete identification in 7:157–158 later performs the same anti-equivocation work: universal authority is encountered through a named bearer, written trace, and specified commission. No later finding materially changes the reading of this brevity as precise relational correction.

## 7:123 — قَالَ فِرْعَوْنُ ءَامَنتُم بِهِۦ قَبْلَ أَنْ ءَاذَنَ لَكُمْ ۖ إِنَّ هَٰذَا لَمَكْرٌۭ مَّكَرْتُمُوهُ فِى ٱلْمَدِينَةِ لِتُخْرِجُوا۟ مِنْهَآ أَهْلَهَا ۖ فَسَوْفَ تَعْلَمُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ء ذ ن:B004` (permission and authorization) is placed before `ء م ن:B002` trusting assent.** Pharaoh treats belief as an act whose validity depends on his prior license. I infer political authority claims ownership of inward judgment. His outrage exposes a permission monopoly, not an evidentiary rebuttal.
2. **`ق ب ل:B002` (priorness) makes sequence the alleged offense.** They believed “before” permission, just as they earlier negotiated casting order. I infer autonomous recognition breaks the ruler's temporal control over public response. Prematurity, not falsehood, is what he names first.
3. **`م د ن:B001` (fortified city), `خ ر ج:B002` (causing out), and `ء ه ل:B001` (people bound by belonging).** Pharaoh reuses the court's earlier projection that Moses seeks expulsion and now expands it into a citywide conspiracy. I infer unlicensed belief → reframed territorial threat → coercive response. The city is treated as a container whose inhabitants the ruler alone may allocate.
4. **No additional `م ك ر` branch is retained.** As at 7:99, the packet inventory lacks a plotting branch that can support this construction; its listed material branches do not form a visible local mechanism. The retained analysis remains anchored to authorization, sequence, city, and expulsion.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayat 7:182–183 later state a gradual, knowledge-asymmetric plan explicitly, contrasting with Pharaoh's unsupported conspiracy attribution. That narrative contrast clarifies his accusation as projection, but it still does not authorize adding a `م ك ر` lexical branch absent from the packet; the original non-activation remains necessary.

## 7:124 — لَأُقَطِّعَنَّ أَيْدِيَكُمْ وَأَرْجُلَكُم مِّنْ خِلَٰفٍۢ ثُمَّ لَأُصَلِّبَنَّكُمْ أَجْمَعِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ق ط ع:B001` (cutting off and severing) targets `ي د ي:B009` (hands as one's acts) and `ر ج ل:B001` (legs).** The threatened mutilation disables both productive action and movement. I infer the regime answers autonomous belief by removing embodied agency. Coercion attacks the capacities through which allegiance can be enacted.
2. **`خ ل ف:B004` (each takes a way other than the other's) sharpens crosswise cutting.** Opposed limbs are severed in differing orientation, making bodily asymmetry a display. I infer dissent is punished by forcibly inscribing contradiction onto the body. The political “different way” becomes anatomical.
3. **`ص ل ب:B005` (execution by fastening) with `ج م ع:B001` (gathering all into one whole).** After dismemberment, all are collectively fixed for public display. I infer scattered bodily capacity → centralized spectacle of state firmness (`ص ل ب:B001`). The ruler tries to restore social cohesion through terror.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:195 later tests false gods by asking whether they have feet to walk, hands to seize, eyes to see, or ears to hear. That inventory unexpectedly sharpens Pharaoh's threat: his rule tries to disable in responsive humans the very agencies his worshipped substitutes cannot demonstrate, then display immobilized bodies as proof of power.

## 7:125 — قَالُوٓا۟ إِنَّآ إِلَىٰ رَبِّنَا مُنقَلِبُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ق ل ب:B005` (return and destination) redirects Pharaoh's bodily threat toward their Lord.** He controls the near route of execution but not the endpoint they name. I infer destination-framing reduces coercion's claim to finality. Their return outruns the ruler's spatial enclosure.
2. **`ق ل ب:B004` (turning and reversing) continues the reversal begun in 7:119.** Court servants became prostrating believers; threatened death is now interpreted as return rather than defeat. I infer transformed inward orientation → transformed meaning of the same external event. Coercion cannot restore the old allegiance.
3. **`ر ب ب:B001` identifies the destination as governing relation.** They do not merely say “we die”; they return to the authority whose signs they recognized. I infer lordship persists across bodily disassembly. The terse answer neutralizes Pharaoh's claim over completion.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:185 later directs attention to the dominion and an approaching term, and 7:196 names God as patron of the sound. These findings confirm that destination and governing relation, not Pharaoh's immediate control of bodies, establish the final frame of the threatened event.

## 7:126 — وَمَا تَنقِمُ مِنَّآ إِلَّآ أَنْ ءَامَنَّا بِـَٔايَٰتِ رَبِّنَا لَمَّا جَآءَتْنَا ۚ رَبَّنَآ أَفْرِغْ عَلَيْنَا صَبْرًۭا وَتَوَفَّنَا مُسْلِمِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ن ق م:B001` (rejecting and faulting something) identifies trusting the arrived signs as the sole fault.** The exclusivity exposes punishment as retaliation against evidence-responsive belief, not conspiracy proof. I infer Pharaoh's governance treats independent recognition itself as defect.
2. **`ف ر غ:B002` (pouring out and emptying a vessel) acts on `ص ب ر:B001` (self-restraint from panic).** They ask that patience be poured over them under the threat of cutting and fastening. I infer abundant external supply → internal containment under bodily assault. Patience is pictured as a covering volume, not self-generated stoicism.
3. **`و ف ي:B002` (receiving the soul in full as death) with `س ل م:B012` (handing over and letting go), alongside `B001` soundness.** They ask for complete reception while in a state of surrendered soundness. I infer bodily severance need not fragment the final person received. Death becomes full transfer.
4. **`ء م ن:B002` responds immediately when signs `ج ي ء:B001` arrive.** Unlike Pharaoh's demanded permission delay, their trust follows evidence without institutional lag. I infer timely reception is the act the regime cannot tolerate.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayat 7:156–157 later join expansive mercy, caution, trust, following, and success; 7:196 adds divine patronage. The later findings confirm poured patience as supplied protection for sustained assent, while the messenger's limits in 7:188 keep that supply attached to the Lord rather than the human sign-bearer.

## 7:127 — وَقَالَ ٱلْمَلَأُ مِن قَوْمِ فِرْعَوْنَ أَتَذَرُ مُوسَىٰ وَقَوْمَهُۥ لِيُفْسِدُوا۟ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ وَيَذَرَكَ وَءَالِهَتَكَ ۚ قَالَ سَنُقَتِّلُ أَبْنَآءَهُمْ وَنَسْتَحْىِۦ نِسَآءَهُمْ وَإِنَّا فَوْقَهُمْ قَٰهِرُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`و ذ ر:B003` (leaving or letting be) is repeated as the elite's central fear.** Letting Moses' community exist freely is equated with corruption and abandonment of Pharaoh and his gods. I infer the regime experiences non-control as social decay. Freedom itself is reclassified as destructive.
2. **`ق ت ل:B001` (putting to death) targets `ب ن ي:B007` filiation, while `ح ي ي:B006` (sparing alive) selectively retains `ن س و:B001` women.** I infer demographic selection → interruption of future autonomous lineage while preserving exploitable survivors. The policy controls continuity, not only present dissent.
3. **`ف و ق:B003` (overpowering superiority) joins `ق ه ر:B001` (overpowering from above that subdues).** The closing claim supplies the vertical model behind killing and sparing. I infer asserted above-position → entitlement to manage another people's life-cycle. Political rank becomes embodied domination.
4. **`ف س د:B001` (departure from balance) is projected onto Moses while the proposed response destroys demographic balance.** I infer the regime names corrective liberation “corruption” to authorize actual systemic damage. The label reverses agent and harm.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:157 later defines the messenger's work as removing burdens and shackles, directly rebutting the regime's label of corruption. Ayat 7:194–196 then reduce invoked rivals to fellow servants without effective limbs and locate real patronage elsewhere, exposing Pharaoh's vertical domination as unsupported imitation of lordship.

## 7:128 — قَالَ مُوسَىٰ لِقَوْمِهِ ٱسْتَعِينُوا۟ بِٱللَّهِ وَٱصْبِرُوٓا۟ ۖ إِنَّ ٱلْأَرْضَ لِلَّهِ يُورِثُهَا مَن يَشَآءُ مِنْ عِبَادِهِۦ ۖ وَٱلْعَٰقِبَةُ لِلْمُتَّقِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ع و ن:B001` (helping and backing) with `ص ب ر:B001` (self-restraint from panic).** Against immediate coercive force, the response combines external backing and internal temporal endurance. I infer help sustains restraint until ownership conditions change. Neither root alone supplies the full survival mechanism.
2. **`و ر ث:B001` (possession passing to heirs) relocates `ء ر ض:B001` ownership from Pharaoh to divine allocation.** 7:127's “above them” claim and 7:137's actual inheritance frame the contrast. I infer present possession is provisional under a larger transfer system. Land is not proof of permanent legitimacy.
3. **`ع ق ب:B006` (end and outcome) belongs to `و ق ي:B002` protective caution.** Patience is oriented toward the rear result rather than immediate pain. I infer guarded conduct through coercion → eventual favorable outcome. Taqwa has temporal architecture.
4. **`ع ب د:B003` (submissive obedience) identifies potential inheritors as servants, not new absolute owners.** I infer transfer changes custodians while preserving higher ownership. Succession is itself another obedience test.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:137 later realizes every major relation here: the made-weak inherit the blessed land because they endured, while Pharaoh's works are dismantled. The calf episode immediately afterward supplies the qualification already latent in “servants”: transfer of land does not transfer absolute ownership or end the successor's test.

## 7:129 — قَالُوٓا۟ أُوذِينَا مِن قَبْلِ أَن تَأْتِيَنَا وَمِنۢ بَعْدِ مَا جِئْتَنَا ۚ قَالَ عَسَىٰ رَبُّكُمْ أَن يُهْلِكَ عَدُوَّكُمْ وَيَسْتَخْلِفَكُمْ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ فَيَنظُرَ كَيْفَ تَعْمَلُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ء ذ ي:B001` (unsettling disliked harm) spans `ق ب ل:B002` before and `ب ع د:B002` after messenger-arrival.** The people observe no immediate discontinuity in suffering. I infer arrival of guidance changes meaning and future possibility before it necessarily changes pressure. The messenger is not a short-term pain switch.
2. **`خ ل ف:B001` (standing in another's place as successor) follows possible enemy destruction.** The promised reversal makes the oppressed future holders of the same earth. I infer liberation → transfer of power-role, not exemption from testing. Succession can reproduce the predecessor unless practice changes.
3. **`ن ظ ر:B001` (directing sight/insight) acts on `ع م ل:B001` (intentional work).** The final clause makes inherited power an observational trial: how they work will be seen. I infer removal of oppressor → exposure of successor agency. Outcome becomes a new input to audit.
4. **`ع س ي:B006` (hoped return of milk), retained exploratorily in `عَسَىٰ`.** The local deprivation and fruit-shortage sequence makes hoped return of provision an apt material echo. I infer hope concerns restored generative flow, though the modal surface does not directly denote milk.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:137 confirms the hoped transfer, but 7:138–152 immediately records imitation, a manufactured calf, and failed delegated leadership. The later sequence strongly validates the final clause as the promise's governing term: liberation changes who can act, then exposes what the successor does with that capacity.

## 7:130 — وَلَقَدْ أَخَذْنَآ ءَالَ فِرْعَوْنَ بِٱلسِّنِينَ وَنَقْصٍۢ مِّنَ ٱلثَّمَرَٰتِ لَعَلَّهُمْ يَذَّكَّرُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`س ن و:B008` (a year and staying a year) with `ن ق ص:B001` (loss of part and decrease).** Prolonged time and reduced fruit turn ecological supply into sustained pressure. I infer duration + diminishing output → disruption of regime confidence. The test targets continuity, not one shocking event.
2. **`ث م ر:B001` (ripe tree fruit) and `B003` (fruit of benefit).** Shortage reaches both literal yield and the benefit produced by Pharaoh's claimed order. I infer reduced harvest → visible failure of the ruler's control narrative. Economic/ecological consequence becomes political evidence.
3. **`ء خ ذ:B007` (a condition taking hold in the body) with `ذ ك ر:B003` (bringing to mind).** The years seize the household so that memory may reactivate. I infer embodied deprivation → recall of prior signs and covenant. Pressure aims at cognition before terminal requital.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:168 later explicitly calls good and bad conditions tests “so that they might return,” matching the purpose of remembrance here. Ayah 7:201 shows remembrance restoring sight after suggestion. These findings confirm sustained scarcity as an attempted cognitive reopening before it is read as terminal requital.

## 7:131 — فَإِذَا جَآءَتْهُمُ ٱلْحَسَنَةُ قَالُوا۟ لَنَا هَٰذِهِۦ ۖ وَإِن تُصِبْهُمْ سَيِّئَةٌۭ يَطَّيَّرُوا۟ بِمُوسَىٰ وَمَن مَّعَهُۥٓ ۗ أَلَآ إِنَّمَا طَٰٓئِرُهُمْ عِندَ ٱللَّهِ وَلَٰكِنَّ أَكْثَرَهُمْ لَا يَعْلَمُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ح س ن:B001` good is claimed as “ours,” while `ص و ب:B004` affliction that hits is assigned to Moses.** The asymmetry mirrors 7:95's misreading of alternating adversity and ease. I infer self-credit for benefit + external blame for harm → impermeable causal model. No input can correct the regime.
2. **`ط ي ر:B001` (airborne lightness/birds), retained in the omen construction, depicts causality being made to fly toward Moses and his companions.** I infer blame is detached from the regime's acts and projected onto a nearby bearer. The branch inventory lacks an explicit omen image, so the flight-mechanism is exploratory but visibly supported by the surface form.
3. **`ع ن د:B004` (proximity and presence at something) relocates their “bird/lot” with Allah.** I infer actual causal allocation lies outside their propaganda assignment. The correction recenters authority over outcomes.
4. **`ع ل م:B001` (knowing as clear), negated for most.** Repeated good/bad data are present, but their attribution rule blocks learning. I infer biased assignment → persistent ignorance despite observations.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:168 confirms both good and bad as one assay, while 7:189–190 repeats the attribution failure at household scale: after a sound child is granted, recipients assign partners in the gift. The later parallel strengthens the diagnosis of self-credit and displaced causality as a reusable defense against learning.

## 7:132 — وَقَالُوا۟ مَهْمَا تَأْتِنَا بِهِۦ مِنْ ءَايَةٍۢ لِّتَسْحَرَنَا بِهَا فَمَا نَحْنُ لَكَ بِمُؤْمِنِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ء ي ي:B003` (visible sign) is preclassified as `س ح ر:B002` (diversion/deception) before any future sign arrives.** I infer a fixed category absorbs every possible datum. The statement is an announced non-falsifiability mechanism.
2. **`ء ت ي:B002` (bringing) is universalized by “whatever.”** No content Moses brings can alter the receiver rule. I infer the delivery channel is accepted physically but blocked epistemically. This is the stamped-heart process of 7:100–101 spoken aloud.
3. **`ء م ن:B002` (trusting assent), categorically negated “for you.”** Their refusal attaches to messenger relation rather than evidence quality. I infer hostility to the source controls evaluation of all signs. Non-belief is pledged in advance.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:146 later restates this policy structurally: even if every sign is seen, the self-magnifying do not trust it or take the sound path. Ayah 7:201 nevertheless shows suggestion interrupted by remembrance, so announced non-falsifiability describes an entrenched receiver rule, not a universal impossibility of return.

## 7:133 — فَأَرْسَلْنَا عَلَيْهِمُ ٱلطُّوفَانَ وَٱلْجَرَادَ وَٱلْقُمَّلَ وَٱلضَّفَادِعَ وَٱلدَّمَ ءَايَٰتٍۢ مُّفَصَّلَٰتٍۢ فَٱسْتَكْبَرُوا۟ وَكَانُوا۟ قَوْمًۭا مُّجْرِمِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ر س ل:B001` (sending forth) dispatches `ط و ف:B002` (enveloping flood/event), `ج ر د:B004` (locusts stripping vegetation), `ق م ل:B001` (tiny vermin), `ض ف د ع:B001` (frogs), and `د م و:B001` (blood).** The signs move from environmental envelope through crop, tiny infestation, animal presence, and bodily fluid. I infer multi-scale penetration → closure of ordinary escape by changing domains. Dispatch forms a distributed pressure system.
2. **`ف ص ل:B013` (detailing that clarifies parts and meanings) keeps the signs distinct rather than one undifferentiated calamity.** I infer separated episodes create repeated opportunities to map promise, relief, and breach. Detail serves diagnosis and memory.
3. **`ك ب ر:B006` (self-magnification) persists across changing scales.** Even tiny vermin and intimate blood do not lower the regime. I infer rank-defense is substrate-independent; it reclassifies every intrusion. Their response, not sign size, is invariant.
4. **`ج ر م:B003` (earning and incurring) frames the community after the detailed series.** I infer repeated rejection accumulates liability across episodes. Criminality is the produced sum of responses.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:146 confirms that self-magnification can survive sight of every sign, while 7:174 states that detailed turning of signs aims at return. The later findings sharpen detail as repeated diagnostic opportunity: separated signals make response traceable but cannot by themselves force a lowered posture.

## 7:134 — وَلَمَّا وَقَعَ عَلَيْهِمُ ٱلرِّجْزُ قَالُوا۟ يَٰمُوسَى ٱدْعُ لَنَا رَبَّكَ بِمَا عَهِدَ عِندَكَ ۖ لَئِن كَشَفْتَ عَنَّا ٱلرِّجْزَ لَنُؤْمِنَنَّ لَكَ وَلَنُرْسِلَنَّ مَعَكَ بَنِىٓ إِسْرَٰٓءِيلَ

### Activated readings

1. **`و ق ع:B001` (falling into fixed reality) makes `ر ج ز:B001` (disturbance and successive motion) personally unavoidable.** Their advance magic-label weakens only when disturbance settles upon them. I infer embodied fixed pressure → temporary reopening of speech. Suffering breaks categorical distance.
2. **`د ع و:B001` (calling to draw near) is delegated to Moses through `ع ه د:B003` (kept covenant).** They recognize his maintained relation “with” the Lord when they need removal. I infer acknowledged covenantal access → use of the rejected messenger as mediator. Their practical model contradicts their public magic model.
3. **`ك ش ف:B001` (removing a cover) is requested for the disturbance.** I infer affliction behaves as an enveloping layer whose removal would expose ordinary life again. Uncovering becomes the condition for promised trust and release.
4. **`ر س ل:B001` links their promise to Moses' original demand.** If the cover is lifted, they will release the Israelites with him. I infer relief is exchanged for relinquishing containment. The agreement directly revisits 7:105.

### Retrospective surprises

The scripture-heirs in 7:169 later repeat the conditional transaction: they take immediate gain, say forgiveness will follow, and would take another similar offer despite a truth-covenant. That parallel confirms the promise here as pressure-dependent bargaining rather than maintained assent.

## 7:135 — فَلَمَّا كَشَفْنَا عَنْهُمُ ٱلرِّجْزَ إِلَىٰٓ أَجَلٍ هُم بَٰلِغُوهُ إِذَا هُمْ يَنكُثُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ك ش ف:B001` (removing a cover) restores space only until `ء ج ل:B001` (fixed term).** Relief is bounded, not proof that warning disappeared. I infer temporary uncovering → test of covenant without pressure. The interval reveals what fear had temporarily concealed about their intention.
2. **`ب ل غ:B001` (reaching the limit) makes the term an endpoint they will inevitably meet.** Earlier Moses' message reached them; now they themselves move toward a set limit. I infer relief does not stop temporal approach. The open interval still has direction.
3. **`ن ك ث:B001` (undoing firm weaving) and `B002` (breaking a binding covenant).** Their promise had tied belief and release to relief; once relief comes they unweave that relation. I infer removal of external tension → exposure of unmaintained internal bond. Breach is a textile disassembly.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayat 7:169–171 later return to a studied covenant, repair, and firm taking under a mountain, exposing what unweaving lacks: a bond maintained after immediate pressure changes. The gradual respite of 7:182–183 also confirms that an apparently open interval can still move steadily toward a fixed accounting point.

## 7:136 — فَٱنتَقَمْنَا مِنْهُمْ فَأَغْرَقْنَٰهُمْ فِى ٱلْيَمِّ بِأَنَّهُمْ كَذَّبُوا۟ بِـَٔايَٰتِنَا وَكَانُوا۟ عَنْهَا غَٰفِلِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ن ق م:B002` (punitive requital) and `غ ر ق:B001` (drowning) place them inside `ي م م:B003` (sea/great water).** The same people repeatedly requested covers removed and refused release; now water becomes an unremoved enclosing medium. I infer covenant breach + denial → terminal containment. Drowning reverses their political containment of Israel.
2. **`ي م م:B001` (aiming deliberately toward something) adds a secondary directional image.** Their repeated chosen response moves toward the sea-outcome even while each relief seems to move away from it. I infer cumulative choices establish an aim-like trajectory. This branch is contextual, not a claim of conscious desire for drowning.
3. **`غ ف ل:B004` (deliberate heedlessness or studied disregard) accompanies `ك ذ ب:B002` denial.** Their heedlessness follows detailed signs, explicit promises, and relief episodes. I infer attention is actively withdrawn from known evidence. The outcome is not caused by informational scarcity.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:146 directly joins denial to deliberate disregard despite comprehensive sight, and 7:179 generalizes heedlessness as unused hearts, eyes, and ears. These later findings confirm that terminal containment follows a maintained attention-policy across detailed opportunities, not a single uninformed lapse.

## 7:137 — وَأَوْرَثْنَا ٱلْقَوْمَ ٱلَّذِينَ كَانُوا۟ يُسْتَضْعَفُونَ مَشَٰرِقَ ٱلْأَرْضِ وَمَغَٰرِبَهَا ٱلَّتِى بَٰرَكْنَا فِيهَا ۖ وَتَمَّتْ كَلِمَتُ رَبِّكَ ٱلْحُسْنَىٰ عَلَىٰ بَنِىٓ إِسْرَٰٓءِيلَ بِمَا صَبَرُوا۟ ۖ وَدَمَّرْنَا مَا كَانَ يَصْنَعُ فِرْعَوْنُ وَقَوْمُهُۥ وَمَا كَانُوا۟ يَعْرِشُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`و ر ث:B001` transfers land to those subjected to `ض ع ف:B001` weakness, spanning `ش ر ق:B001` east-rising and `غ ر ب:B006` west-setting.** The full solar arc replaces Pharaoh's claim to be above them. I infer endurance through domination → comprehensive territorial reversal. Inheritance expands from captive locality to horizon.
2. **`ب ر ك:B004` (fixed, growing good) combines with `ت م م:B001` (full completion) of `ك ل م:B002` (meaningful word).** Promise becomes spatially productive reality, completing the speech-action chain. I infer patient endurance (`ص ب ر:B001`) carries the word to its full allotment. Completion is both semantic fulfillment and material settlement.
3. **`د م ر:B002` (destructive ruin) acts on `ص ن ع:B001` (made action) and `ع ر ش:B002/B003` raised roofs/trellises.** Pharaoh's manufactured and elevated order is dismantled while the formerly weak inherit. I infer coercive construction depends on a political support relation that can be removed. Technical production does not secure legitimacy.
4. **`ص ب ر:B001` supplies the stated causal condition.** The packet gives inheritance “because they endured”; I infer endurance preserved group continuity until transfer. Patience is the bridge between promise and completion.

### Retrospective surprises

The immediate calf sequence and the later scripture-heirs of 7:169 prevent inheritance from being read as moral certification of the inheritor. The transfer is real and endurance causally matters, but received land and completed promise become new entrusted conditions whose use must still be audited.

## 7:138 — وَجَٰوَزْنَا بِبَنِىٓ إِسْرَٰٓءِيلَ ٱلْبَحْرَ فَأَتَوْا۟ عَلَىٰ قَوْمٍۢ يَعْكُفُونَ عَلَىٰٓ أَصْنَامٍۢ لَّهُمْ ۚ قَالُوا۟ يَٰمُوسَى ٱجْعَل لَّنَآ إِلَٰهًۭا كَمَا لَهُمْ ءَالِهَةٌۭ ۚ قَالَ إِنَّكُمْ قَوْمٌۭ تَجْهَلُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ج و ز:B002` (crossing and passing through) `ب ح ر:B001` (wide abundant water) is followed by `ع ك ف:B001` (turning toward and staying with) idols.** The delivered group moves through the great barrier only to admire another group's stationary attachment. I infer physical passage does not guarantee relational passage beyond prior authority habits. Motion and fixation collide.
2. **`ع ك ف:B004` (being gathered around a thing) with `ص ن م:B001` (idol-body).** The observed community organizes itself around visible bodies; Israel asks Moses to manufacture an equivalent center. I infer visible social cohesion → mimetic desire for its object. They seek the group's organizing hardware.
3. **`ج ع ل:B001` (bringing/making into being) applied to `ء ل ه:B001` worship-object.** The request exposes the inversion of manufactured deity: they ask a messenger to make what would then govern them. I infer desire for tangible authority → attempt to produce its source. The causal order is incoherent.
4. **`ج ه ل:B002` (rashness and unsettled folly), alongside `B001` lack of recognition.** Immediate imitation after deliverance shows failure to recognize what crossing demonstrated about lordship. I infer novelty encounter + unprocessed liberation → rash copying.

### Retrospective surprises

The calf in 7:148 later realizes the requested manufactured center, while 7:191–195 subjects such objects to a functional test: they create nothing, help no one, and lack effective limbs and senses. The later material confirms that physical crossing without source-recognition leaves the organizing habits of captivity available for imitation.

## 7:139 — إِنَّ هَٰٓؤُلَآءِ مُتَبَّرٌۭ مَّا هُمْ فِيهِ وَبَٰطِلٌۭ مَّا كَانُوا۟ يَعْمَلُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ت ب ر:B001` (destruction, nullification, and breaking) governs the condition “they are in.”** Their ritual enclosure around idols appears stable, but its containing relation is headed toward breakage. I infer attachment to a made center → structurally brittle social world. Destruction is internal to the arrangement's false support.
2. **`ب ط ل:B001` (voidness and lack of truth) attaches to `ع م ل:B001` (intentional work).** Like Pharaoh's magicians, the idol community performs real repeated labor whose truth-bearing value is void. I infer persistence and social coordination do not validate an object's authority. Work can maintain unreality.
3. **The two passive/result constructions separate process from outcome.** The packet supplies existing practice and destined nullification; I infer continued work cannot repair a false source relation. The ayah reads as a systems prognosis.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:147 later generalizes voided work under denial, and 7:191–198 demonstrates the idol system's missing functions in detail. These findings strongly confirm the prognosis: coordinated, repeated labor can sustain a visible institution while remaining unable to supply creation, guidance, aid, or answer.

## 7:140 — قَالَ أَغَيْرَ ٱللَّهِ أَبْغِيكُمْ إِلَٰهًۭا وَهُوَ فَضَّلَكُمْ عَلَى ٱلْعَٰلَمِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ب غ ي:B001` (seeking and pursuing) an alternative (`غ ي ر:B005`) worship-object.** Moses frames their request as commissioning him to search for a substitute authority. I infer imitation has turned delivered people into consumers in a deity market. The rhetorical question exposes the procurement logic.
2. **`ف ض ل:B003` (beneficence and bounty) with `B002` rank/virtue.** They were favored through signs, release, inheritance, and crossing, yet seek equivalence with the stationary idol group. I infer unremembered favor → downward comparison and substitute desire. Privilege creates responsibility to recognize source, not entitlement to collect gods.
3. **`ع ل م:B002` (distinguishing mark/worlds).** Their deliverance marked them among peoples; asking for the observed group's center erases that learned distinction. I infer favor should produce differentiated allegiance. The request tries to remove the very mark bestowed by history.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayat 7:156–157 later tie mercy and success to following the messenger and descended light, converting favor into accountable direction. Ayat 7:194–196 then show invoked alternatives to be fellow servants without effective help and locate patronage with God, closing the substitute-authority market exposed here.

## 7:141 — وَإِذْ أَنجَيْنَٰكُم مِّنْ ءَالِ فِرْعَوْنَ يَسُومُونَكُمْ سُوٓءَ ٱلْعَذَابِ ۖ يُقَتِّلُونَ أَبْنَآءَكُمْ وَيَسْتَحْيُونَ نِسَآءَكُمْ ۚ وَفِى ذَٰلِكُم بَلَآءٌۭ مِّن رَّبِّكُمْ عَظِيمٌۭ

### Activated readings

1. **`ن ج و:B001` (deliverance into separation) answers `س و م:B002` (imposing injury).** The rescue is not an abstract favor: it extracts the people from a regime repeatedly assigning them harmful treatment. I infer the remembered source of freedom should govern the allegiance question in 7:140. Seeking another deity immediately after extraction forgets who actually separated them from Pharaoh's house.
2. **The paired policies—killing sons and preserving women—make `ب ل و:B002` (testing that discloses) social and generational.** The coercion attacks continuity while retaining a controlled remainder. I infer the trial reveals whether communal identity can survive a power that decides who may live. The great test is therefore not reducible to private pain.
3. **`ب ل و:B003` (a beneficial trial/favor) remains possible beside `B002`.** “In that” can gather both the oppression just recalled and the deliverance opening the ayah. I infer one history may test under harm and disclose benefaction through rescue; the branch pairing changes the reading from a single-valence calamity to a severe revelatory passage.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:157 later characterizes the messenger's work as removing burdens and shackles, while 7:168 explicitly assays communities through both good and bad conditions. These findings confirm the recalled history's double disclosure: imposed generational harm reveals domination, and rescue reveals a benefaction that must alter allegiance.

## 7:142 — ۞ وَوَٰعَدْنَا مُوسَىٰ ثَلَٰثِينَ لَيْلَةًۭ وَأَتْمَمْنَٰهَا بِعَشْرٍۢ فَتَمَّ مِيقَٰتُ رَبِّهِۦٓ أَرْبَعِينَ لَيْلَةًۭ ۚ وَقَالَ مُوسَىٰ لِأَخِيهِ هَٰرُونَ ٱخْلُفْنِى فِى قَوْمِى وَأَصْلِحْ وَلَا تَتَّبِعْ سَبِيلَ ٱلْمُفْسِدِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`و ق ت:B003` (an appointed temporal station) is completed through `ت م م:B001` (reaching wholeness): thirty nights plus ten become forty.** The packet gives staged completion, not an unexplained absence. I infer the later calf is formed inside a known but extended waiting structure; impatience converts a bounded appointment into perceived vacancy.
2. **`خ ل ف:B001` (succeeding or taking another's place) defines Aaron's role.** Moses delegates presence among his people while pairing succession with `ص ل ح:B001` (restoring soundness). I infer delegated leadership is custodial: occupy the absent leader's position by repairing communal coherence, not by reproducing his personal authority.
3. **The negative instruction joins `ت ب ع:B003` (following a trace step by step), `س ب ل:B001` (a traversable way), and `ف س د:B001` (departure from balanced soundness).** Corruption is represented as a route that can recruit a successor. I infer office alone does not secure fidelity; Aaron must continually refuse the path by which imbalance propagates.

### Retrospective surprises

Aaron's testimony in 7:150 later qualifies the failed succession: the community weakened him and nearly killed him, so delegated office did not equal coercive capacity. Ayat 7:169–170 then repeat bad succession versus book-holding repair, confirming custodial maintenance rather than title as the relevant measure.

## 7:143 — وَلَمَّا جَآءَ مُوسَىٰ لِمِيقَٰتِنَا وَكَلَّمَهُۥ رَبُّهُۥ قَالَ رَبِّ أَرِنِىٓ أَنظُرْ إِلَيْكَ ۚ قَالَ لَن تَرَىٰنِى وَلَٰكِنِ ٱنظُرْ إِلَى ٱلْجَبَلِ فَإِنِ ٱسْتَقَرَّ مَكَانَهُۥ فَسَوْفَ تَرَىٰنِى ۚ فَلَمَّا تَجَلَّىٰ رَبُّهُۥ لِلْجَبَلِ جَعَلَهُۥ دَكًّۭا وَخَرَّ مُوسَىٰ صَعِقًۭا ۚ فَلَمَّآ أَفَاقَ قَالَ سُبْحَٰنَكَ تُبْتُ إِلَيْكَ وَأَنَا۠ أَوَّلُ ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ك ل م:B001` supplies meaningful exchanged speech before Moses asks for `ر ء ي:B001` direct sight.** The sequence distinguishes two modes of access: speech has occurred, yet visual inspection is neither entailed nor available on demand. I infer genuine relation need not collapse source into an inspectable object—the precise error made by the calf-builders.
2. **The mountain's proposed `ق ر ر:B003` settled stability is tested by `ج ل و:B001` manifestation.** The result, `د ك ك:B001` crushing and leveling, overturns the apparent hierarchy of capacities: even the massive fixed landmark cannot hold its place. I infer the mountain functions as a capacity demonstration, not merely scenery; created stability fails under disclosure.
3. **`خ ر ر:B001` (a sudden downward fall) and `ص ع ق:B003` (being struck senseless) bodily register that failure of capacity.** On recovery, `س ب ح:B002` declares the source free of the limit implicit in the request, and `ء م ن:B002` marks renewed assent. I infer the episode changes Moses' reading from “speech may be extended into sight” to “received relation must respect the asymmetry disclosure itself reveals.”

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:171 later reuses the mountain as a displaced canopy expected to fall while the people are commanded to take and remember firmly. That later scene extends the capacity demonstration into covenant pedagogy: created mass can materialize seriousness, but it cannot contain or domesticate the source.

## 7:144 — قَالَ يَٰمُوسَىٰٓ إِنِّى ٱصْطَفَيْتُكَ عَلَى ٱلنَّاسِ بِرِسَٰلَٰتِى وَبِكَلَٰمِى فَخُذْ مَآ ءَاتَيْتُكَ وَكُن مِّنَ ٱلشَّٰكِرِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ص ف و:B002` (selecting a choice portion) is specified by messages and `ك ل م:B001` exchanged speech.** Selection does not authorize unrestricted access; 7:143 has just exposed that boundary. I infer chosenness means assignment through particular channels, not possession of the source.
2. **`ء خ ذ:B001` (taking into firm possession) converts gift into duty.** Moses is told to take what was given rather than continue seeking what was withheld. I infer the correction redirects attention from unavailable vision to entrusted content; vocation is measured by handling the gift actually supplied.
3. **`ش ك ر:B001` makes recognition of favor the required enduring state.** In the local window, gratitude contrasts with the people's demand for a fabricated worship-object. I infer gratitude preserves source-memory, whereas ingratitude treats received guidance as insufficient and begins substitute production.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:157 later asks hearers to recognize a written messenger-trace and follow descended light, while 7:170 distinguishes actively holding the Book from merely inheriting it. These findings confirm selection as accountable custody of the supplied channel, not entitlement to unavailable forms of access.

## 7:145 — وَكَتَبْنَا لَهُۥ فِى ٱلْأَلْوَاحِ مِن كُلِّ شَىْءٍۢ مَّوْعِظَةًۭ وَتَفْصِيلًۭا لِّكُلِّ شَىْءٍۢ فَخُذْهَا بِقُوَّةٍۢ وَأْمُرْ قَوْمَكَ يَأْخُذُوا۟ بِأَحْسَنِهَا ۚ سَأُو۟رِيكُمْ دَارَ ٱلْفَٰسِقِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ك ت ب:B002` (inscribing) places admonition in `ل و ح:B003` broad tablets, while `و ع ظ:B001` warns in order to soften and redirect.** The guidance acquires a durable external form after the limits of sight in 7:143. I infer inscription supplies a stable communal memory that need not depend on continuous visionary presence.
2. **`ف ص ل:B013` (detailed clarification) works beside the repeated “every thing.”** The tablets do not merely announce; they distinguish and articulate. I infer the written form is designed for judgment across cases, answering the community's vulnerability to visually compelling but undiscriminating substitutes.
3. **Taking them with `ق و ي:B001` evokes strength assembled like strands in a rope.** Moses must take firmly and command his people to take the best of the instruction. I infer reception is collective, tensile work: robust adherence is made from aligned acts, not from admiring the tablets as objects.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:169 later proves that inheritance and repeated study can coexist with appetite-led conduct; 7:170 adds active holding and prayer, and 7:171 repeats strong taking plus remembrance almost verbatim. The later sequence sharply confirms strength as enacted, memory-governed reception rather than possession of inscribed objects.

## 7:146 — سَأَصْرِفُ عَنْ ءَايَٰتِىَ ٱلَّذِينَ يَتَكَبَّرُونَ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ بِغَيْرِ ٱلْحَقِّ وَإِن يَرَوْا۟ كُلَّ ءَايَةٍۢ لَّا يُؤْمِنُوا۟ بِهَا وَإِن يَرَوْا۟ سَبِيلَ ٱلرُّشْدِ لَا يَتَّخِذُوهُ سَبِيلًۭا وَإِن يَرَوْا۟ سَبِيلَ ٱلْغَىِّ يَتَّخِذُوهُ سَبِيلًۭا ۚ ذَٰلِكَ بِأَنَّهُمْ كَذَّبُوا۟ بِـَٔايَٰتِنَا وَكَانُوا۟ عَنْهَا غَٰفِلِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ص ر ف:B001` (turning or diverting away) answers `ك ب ر:B006` self-magnification without right.** The ayah does not describe a shortage of visible evidence: they can see every sign. I infer arrogant posture bends the relation between observer and sign until more sight yields no assent; diversion is an epistemic consequence of self-placement.
2. **Two instances of `س ب ل:B001` place `ر ش د:B001` (a straight, rightly directed course) against `غ و ي:B001` (straying from the course).** Repeated `ر ء ي:B001` establishes equal perceptual availability, while taking versus not taking supplies the divergence. I infer guidance is not mere route-display; it requires adopting the displayed way as one's path.
3. **Denial is paired with `غ ف ل:B004` deliberate disregard.** That pairing blocks an innocent-ignorance reading of their failure. I infer denial → sustained inattention → selective path-taking → further diversion from signs, a reinforcing loop rather than a single mistaken judgment.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:179 later expands the mechanism into hearts, eyes, and ears that exist but are not used, while 7:193 shows a call to guidance that still produces no following. Ayah 7:201 supplies the important qualification: remembrance can restore sight after suggestion, so the reinforcing loop is grave but still addressed by recollection.

## 7:147 — وَٱلَّذِينَ كَذَّبُوا۟ بِـَٔايَٰتِنَا وَلِقَآءِ ٱلْءَاخِرَةِ حَبِطَتْ أَعْمَٰلُهُمْ ۚ هَلْ يُجْزَوْنَ إِلَّا مَا كَانُوا۟ يَعْمَلُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ح ب ط:B001` depicts harmful grazing that swells the belly, here attached to `ع م ل:B001` intentional works.** The material branch makes an exploratory metaphor available: activity may become visibly enlarged while its intake is ruinous. I infer denial of signs and later encounter lets labor accumulate without life-bearing value; quantity of work cannot correct its governing relation.
2. **`ل ق ي:B001` frames the later life as an encounter they deny, not an abstraction they merely fail to calculate.** I infer their work is malformed because it excludes the meeting in which work becomes answerable. The reading shifts from “deeds disappear arbitrarily” to “deeds lose coherence when their final addressee is denied.”
3. **`ج ز ي:B001` supplies matching recompense, and the rhetorical question limits it to what they kept doing.** Packet fact: recompense corresponds to their work. Inferred arrow: sustained action → fitting return, so nullification is itself an enacted outcome rather than an unrelated penalty.

### Retrospective surprises

The successors in 7:169 later make the temporal deformation concrete: they repeatedly seize passing near goods despite knowing the later dwelling is better. Ayah 7:178 calls the misdirected the losers. These findings confirm that work becomes ruinous through its governing horizon, not through lack of visible scale or effort.

## 7:148 — وَٱتَّخَذَ قَوْمُ مُوسَىٰ مِنۢ بَعْدِهِۦ مِنْ حُلِيِّهِمْ عِجْلًۭا جَسَدًۭا لَّهُۥ خُوَارٌ ۚ أَلَمْ يَرَوْا۟ أَنَّهُۥ لَا يُكَلِّمُهُمْ وَلَا يَهْدِيهِمْ سَبِيلًا ۘ ٱتَّخَذُوهُ وَكَانُوا۟ ظَٰلِمِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ح ل ي:B001` ornament is converted into `ع ج ل:B002` a calf, `ج س د:B001` an assembled body, and `خ و ر:B001` a bellowing voice.** The sequence tracks manufacture from valued material to sensory simulation. I infer the community mistakes accumulated signs of body, sound, and wealth for agency; construction supplies salience, not authority.
2. **The ayah tests the artifact by absent functions: no `ك ل م:B001` meaningful exchange and no `ه د ي:B002` course-leading.** Their own seeing should disclose these absences. I infer a valid guide is recognized by communicative and directional work, whereas the calf only returns sound; spectacle fails the functional test available to ordinary perception.
3. **The doubled `ء خ ذ` marks both fabrication and adoption, while `ع ج ل:B001` haste becomes audible beside the calf branch and Moses' charge of haste in 7:150.** This is a branch-level resonance, not a claim that the noun means haste. It changes the reading by linking the chosen calf to premature closure of a waiting interval: they fill an absent leader's place before the appointed time is complete.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayat 7:191–198 later expand this compact audit function by function: invoked objects create nothing, give no help, cannot follow guidance, and possess no effective feet, hands, eyes, or ears. The later inventory decisively confirms agency by observable work rather than body, sound, ornament, or apparent gaze.

## 7:149 — وَلَمَّا سُقِطَ فِىٓ أَيْدِيهِمْ وَرَأَوْا۟ أَنَّهُمْ قَدْ ضَلُّوا۟ قَالُوا۟ لَئِن لَّمْ يَرْحَمْنَا رَبُّنَا وَيَغْفِرْ لَنَا لَنَكُونَنَّ مِنَ ٱلْخَٰسِرِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`س ق ط:B003` (a lapse or error) is located “in their hands,” the bodily site of making and taking in 7:148.** The idiom and branch together turn realization inward: the failure falls into the very agency that produced it. I infer remorse begins when makers see their own hands not as proof of control but as evidence of misdirection.
2. **`ر ء ي:B002` evaluative seeing now succeeds where `B001` visual seeing failed.** They had seen the calf's incapacity but adopted it; now they perceive that they themselves `ض ل ل:B001` left the path. I infer the decisive sight is reflexive judgment, not inspection of the object.
3. **`ر ح م:B001` mercy and `غ ف ر:B001` protective covering become the only alternative to `خ س ر:B001` loss.** Their conditional confession does not bargain with the value of their work. I infer recognized agency → appeal for restorative protection; the hands responsible for the lapse cannot manufacture their own acquittal.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayat 7:168 and 7:174 retain return as the purpose of testing and detailed signs, while 7:201 shows remembrance converting contact by suggestion into restored sight. These later findings confirm reflexive recognition as a real interruption of misdirection, though 7:190 warns that gift-attribution can fail again in another setting.

## 7:150 — وَلَمَّا رَجَعَ مُوسَىٰٓ إِلَىٰ قَوْمِهِۦ غَضْبَٰنَ أَسِفًۭا قَالَ بِئْسَمَا خَلَفْتُمُونِى مِنۢ بَعْدِىٓ ۖ أَعَجِلْتُمْ أَمْرَ رَبِّكُمْ ۖ وَأَلْقَى ٱلْأَلْوَاحَ وَأَخَذَ بِرَأْسِ أَخِيهِ يَجُرُّهُۥٓ إِلَيْهِ ۚ قَالَ ٱبْنَ أُمَّ إِنَّ ٱلْقَوْمَ ٱسْتَضْعَفُونِى وَكَادُوا۟ يَقْتُلُونَنِى فَلَا تُشْمِتْ بِىَ ٱلْأَعْدَآءَ وَلَا تَجْعَلْنِى مَعَ ٱلْقَوْمِ ٱلظَّٰلِمِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **Moses' `ر ج ع:B001` return exposes a failed `خ ل ف:B001` succession.** The appointment in 7:142 was meant to preserve soundness during absence; “badly you succeeded me” reports the opposite outcome. I infer the crisis tests the difference between formal replacement and effective communal maintenance.
2. **`ع ج ل:B001` haste is directed at the Lord's command, whose `و ق ت:B003` appointment had just reached completion.** The calf's noun activates a different branch, `ع ج ل:B002`, in 7:148. Their proximity does not collapse the senses, but it makes the artifact a material signature of premature closure: they chose the calf because they hurried the command's timing.
3. **Taking Aaron by the `ر ء س:B002` leadership-site and `ج ر ر:B001` dragging him makes Moses' first causal diagnosis bodily visible.** Aaron answers that the people weakened him and nearly killed him. I infer Moses initially locates the failure in the visible deputy, then receives evidence that coercive communal power exceeded the deputy's capacity; leadership accountability requires distinguishing complicity from imposed weakness.
4. **`ش م ت:B001` names enemies' malicious rejoicing at another's misfortune.** Aaron's plea asks Moses not to let internal correction become hostile spectacle or classify him with the wrongdoers. I infer even justified anger must preserve distinctions inside the community, or discipline itself supplies adversaries with a false equivalence.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayat 7:159, 7:164, and 7:168 repeatedly distinguish truth-guides, admonishers, wrongdoers, and sound subsets inside larger peoples. That later pattern validates Aaron's plea as an analytical discipline: corrective anger must identify response classes accurately rather than let visible office or shared membership collapse imposed weakness into complicity.

## 7:151 — قَالَ رَبِّ ٱغْفِرْ لِى وَلِأَخِى وَأَدْخِلْنَا فِى رَحْمَتِكَ ۖ وَأَنتَ أَرْحَمُ ٱلرَّٰحِمِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`غ ف ر:B001` protective covering is requested for both Moses and his brother.** Moses' prayer follows Aaron's differentiating testimony, so the plural repairs the rupture made visible by head-taking and dragging. I infer Moses changes from accusation to shared dependence without erasing their different acts.
2. **`د خ ل:B001` gives mercy (`ر ح م:B001`) a spatial role: “enter us” into it.** The request is not only cancellation of fault but relocation into a containing relation. I infer protective covering addresses the past act, while entrance into mercy supplies the environment in which brotherhood and leadership can continue.
3. **The superlative “most merciful of the merciful” shifts final judgment away from Moses' anger.** Packet fact: Moses appeals to the Lord's unmatched mercy. Inferred arrow: corrected causal judgment → joint petition → restored fraternity under a higher evaluator.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:156 later says mercy encompasses every thing before describing durable enrollment, and 7:196 identifies God as patron of the sound. These findings strengthen the spatial reading: forgiveness repairs a specific rupture, while mercy is the larger governing environment into which repaired fraternity seeks entry.

## 7:152 — إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ ٱتَّخَذُوا۟ ٱلْعِجْلَ سَيَنَالُهُمْ غَضَبٌۭ مِّن رَّبِّهِمْ وَذِلَّةٌۭ فِى ٱلْحَيَوٰةِ ٱلدُّنْيَا ۚ وَكَذَٰلِكَ نَجْزِى ٱلْمُفْتَرِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **`ء خ ذ:B010` (taking something as one's own) clarifies the charge: the decisive act is not merely seeing or fabricating the calf but adopting it.** In contrast to Moses taking the given tablets, they appropriate a made object as authority. I infer two rival receptions structure the scene—firmly receive entrusted instruction, or make and possess a substitute.
2. **`ن ي ل:B002` brings anger within reach, while `ذ ل ل:B001` names abasement in the near life.** The adopted object's visible prestige reverses into social lowering. I infer false elevation of a manufactured authority produces the adopters' diminution; the consequence reaches them in the same public world where they installed it.
3. **`ج ز ي:B001` matching recompense is attached to `ف ر ي:B003` fabricated falsehood.** The calf itself was fabricated, and its authority is a fabrication in the propositional sense. I infer maker and lie mirror one another: those who manufacture an object and manufacture its claim receive a correspondingly constructed humiliation.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:170 later protects the reward of repairers, and 7:194 reduces all invoked substitutes to fellow created servants. Together with the immediate reopening in 7:153, the later findings confirm matching abasement without converting fabricated authority into an unchangeable identity for every former adopter.

## 7:153 — وَٱلَّذِينَ عَمِلُوا۟ ٱلسَّيِّـَٔاتِ ثُمَّ تَابُوا۟ مِنۢ بَعْدِهَا وَءَامَنُوٓا۟ إِنَّ رَبَّكَ مِنۢ بَعْدِهَا لَغَفُورٌۭ رَّحِيمٌۭ

### Activated readings

1. **`ع م ل:B001` marks deliberate evil work, but the structural sequence “then … after it … and trusted” refuses to freeze actors in that work.** The packet's supplied `ت و ب` inventory does not retain an ordinary agent-return branch, so I do not force one; the surface and sequence still establish a post-wrong reorientation. I infer the ayah qualifies 7:152: matching consequence is not a closed identity when action is followed by changed allegiance.
2. **`ء م ن:B002` assent and trust supplies the positive replacement, not mere cessation.** The twice-repeated “after it” stresses temporal succession. I infer repair requires more than regret: harmful work → reorientation → renewed trust.
3. **`غ ف ر:B001` protective covering and `ر ح م:B001` mercy describe the Lord after the actors' changed sequence.** The reading moves from fabricated authority and reached anger to a protected future. Later mercy does not call the earlier work unreal; it prevents the work from becoming the person's final enclosure.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:168 makes return the aim of alternating tests, 7:170 names the resulting positive work as repair, and 7:174 makes detailed signs serve return. Ayah 7:201 then models the transition in miniature: suggestion touches, remembrance occurs, and sight reopens. The later run strongly confirms sequence rather than fixed actor-label.

## 7:154 — وَلَمَّا سَكَتَ عَن مُّوسَى ٱلْغَضَبُ أَخَذَ ٱلْأَلْوَاحَ ۖ وَفِى نُسْخَتِهَا هُدًۭى وَرَحْمَةٌۭ لِّلَّذِينَ هُمْ لِرَبِّهِمْ يَرْهَبُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **`س ك ت:B002` portrays a stirred movement settling, with anger as the grammatical actor that becomes silent.** Only then does Moses retake the tablets. I infer emotional settling restores the capacity to handle guidance; the sequence does not condemn anger's cause, but disciplines its duration and custody of action.
2. **`ء خ ذ:B001` reverses the casting down in 7:150, while `ن س خ:B002` identifies a written transfer or copy in the tablets.** The durable inscription survives the leadership crisis as transmissible guidance. I infer the text can be dropped by an overwhelmed bearer yet remain available for renewed possession; written mediation outlasts momentary dysregulation.
3. **Guidance (`ه د ي:B002`) and mercy (`ر ح م:B001`) are activated for those who `ر ه ب:B003` approach their Lord with devoted awe.** This is not the recoil Pharaoh attempted to induce. I infer the same tablets become life-directing rather than merely external law when received within reverent source-awareness.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:170 later joins active book-holding to established prayer, 7:171 adds strong taking and remembrance, and 7:205 specifies humble, quiet recollection. The full run confirms that recovering a dropped text is only the first step; durable guidance requires regulated affect, recurrent practice, and source-aware memory.

## 7:155 — وَٱخْتَارَ مُوسَىٰ قَوْمَهُۥ سَبْعِينَ رَجُلًۭا لِّمِيقَٰتِنَا ۖ فَلَمَّآ أَخَذَتْهُمُ ٱلرَّجْفَةُ قَالَ رَبِّ لَوْ شِئْتَ أَهْلَكْتَهُم مِّن قَبْلُ وَإِيَّٰىَ ۖ أَتُهْلِكُنَا بِمَا فَعَلَ ٱلسُّفَهَآءُ مِنَّآ ۖ إِنْ هِىَ إِلَّا فِتْنَتُكَ تُضِلُّ بِهَا مَن تَشَآءُ وَتَهْدِى مَن تَشَآءُ ۖ أَنتَ وَلِيُّنَا فَٱغْفِرْ لَنَا وَٱرْحَمْنَا ۖ وَأَنتَ خَيْرُ ٱلْغَٰفِرِينَ

### Activated readings

1. **Moses' choosing activates `خ ي ر:B003` selection of what is judged better, and the seventy enter `و ق ت:B003` an appointed station.** Yet `ر ج ف:B001` violent shaking takes the selected representatives. I infer selection and appointment confer responsibility, not immunity; representative bodies are themselves tested.
2. **`س ف ه:B002` lightness or unsoundness of mind distinguishes “the foolish among us” from the whole group.** Moses' question resists indiscriminate collective destruction while still speaking as “us.” I infer mature representation holds solidarity and moral differentiation together: he does not abandon the group, but he does not flatten all agency into one class.
3. **`ف ت ن:B001` is an assay that discloses quality, and its two outcomes are `ض ل ل:B001` straying and `ه د ي:B002` being set on a course.** Packet fact: the same divine test is related to divergent outcomes by will. Inferred move: the shaking reveals that an event is not self-interpreting; relation to the patron determines whether pressure disorients or directs.
4. **`و ل ي:B003` names governing patronage, followed by `غ ف ر:B001` covering and `ر ح م:B001` mercy.** Moses answers exposed communal fragility by appealing to rightful authority rather than to the selected men's merit. The request relocates security from representative status to the patron's protection.

### Retrospective surprises

Ayah 7:168 later explicitly applies one assay through opposite conditions while preserving sound and unsound subgroups; 7:172 places witness on each self. Ayah 7:196 repeats divine patronage. These findings confirm solidarity under one test without collapsing representative selection, collective location, and individual response into one moral class.

## 7:156 — ۞ وَٱكْتُبْ لَنَا فِى هَٰذِهِ ٱلدُّنْيَا حَسَنَةًۭ وَفِى ٱلْءَاخِرَةِ إِنَّا هُدْنَآ إِلَيْكَ ۚ قَالَ عَذَابِىٓ أُصِيبُ بِهِۦ مَنْ أَشَآءُ ۖ وَرَحْمَتِى وَسِعَتْ كُلَّ شَىْءٍۢ ۚ فَسَأَكْتُبُهَا لِلَّذِينَ يَتَّقُونَ وَيُؤْتُونَ ٱلزَّكَوٰةَ وَٱلَّذِينَ هُم بِـَٔايَٰتِنَا يُؤْمِنُونَ

### Activated readings

1. **The repeated `ك ت ب:B003` decree answers Moses' request for good in the near and later lives.** Mercy first `و س ع:B001` encompasses every thing, then is written for a described people. I infer breadth and enrollment are not opposites: universal reach names mercy's capacity, while the following practices describe how a community enters its durable allocation.
